### [Maybe We Shouldn't Be Reviewing All This Code](https://martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings/code-review.html)

**来源**: Martin Fowler

**摘要**: <p class="precis"><b>TL;DR</b><br /><i>Or, perhaps the problem isn't that AI has broken code review, maybe it’s that we've been using code review to solve the wrong problems</i></p>
<img src="https://martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings/card.png" /><p>I was on a panel recently with Brian Houck from DX at Code Remix, hosted by Moderne. It was one of the more interesting panels I’ve done, largely because we disagreed. As my colleague Martin Fowler says, panels are much more interesting when people disagree and both sides have a good argument. Brian and I definitely did.</p>

<p>Brian has since written a thoughtful piece called <a href="https://newsletter.getdx.com/p/what-are-code-reviews-even-for"><em>What are code reviews even for?</em></a> He is clearly passionate about his position, and I am passionate enough about mine that I’m writing this response. To be clear, I think we mostly want the same things. I just don’t think code review is the best way to get them. Brian is lovely, by the way, and encouraged me to write this. But I’d be lying if I said I didn’t want you to think I’m right by the end :)</p>

<p>So what were we disagreeing about?</p>

<p>AI is producing more code than humans can realistically review. Brian cites some pretty striking numbers: at Meta, significant lines of code per human-landed diff reportedly increased 106% in a year, while DX’s own data shows median pull request size increasing 64%.</p>

<p>His concern, which I share, is that simply automating code review away risks losing all the other things we use it for. Code review isn’t just about finding bugs. It’s how teams share knowledge, teach junior engineers, build collective ownership and spread architectural understanding.</p>

<p>My question is: <strong>why are we waiting until code review to do all of those things?</strong></p>

<p>I’ve never particularly liked pull requests as the centre of the software development process. Not because engineers shouldn’t look at each other’s code, but because I’ve always struggled with the idea that we should build something, finish it, package it up, throw it over to somebody else and <em>then</em> have the important conversation about whether we built the right thing in the right way.</p>

<p>And don’t even get me started on merge conflicts. I’ve lost too many hours of my life.</p>

<h2 id="shift-the-judgment-left"><strong>Shift the judgment left</strong></h2>

<p>One of the principles I learned very early at Thoughtworks was to shorten feedback loops. If feedback is valuable, don’t remove it. Move it closer to the decision it is informing.</p>

<p>Take the things we say code review gives us.</p>

<p>If we want to <strong>explore alternative solutions</strong>, I’d rather do that before implementing one of them.</p>

<p>If we want <strong>knowledge transfer</strong>, pair. Sitting next to someone, physically or virtually, while they reason through a problem teaches you far more than reading their completed solution afterwards.</p>

<p>If we want <strong>junior engineers to learn how experienced engineers think</strong>, let them work with experienced engineers while they’re thinking. Pairing comes to mind again here, but teams could also do design sessions collectively with a whiteboard before they write (or instruct the agent to write) anything.</p>

<p>If we want <strong>collective ownership</strong>, organise teams so people actually build and operate software collectively rather than relying on a pull request to tell everyone what somebody else has already built. For this again use pairing, mob programming, or team design sessions around whiteboard.</p>

<p>If we want <strong>architectural alignment</strong>, design together (I won’t repeat myself about pairing and team design sessions, oh wait…) and then encode the important constraints as fitness functions.</p>

<p>And if we’re reviewing code for formatting, linting, known security problems or things that can be deterministically tested, automate them. We really shouldn’t still be arguing about whitespace in 2026.</p>

<p>Pair programming, trunk-based development, automated testing, static analysis, fitness functions and security scanning all move feedback earlier. Increasingly, agents can participate in those loops too, challenging designs, testing assumptions and continuously verifying what is being built, but the real thinking is coming from experienced humans and if we want that experience to benefit the whole team then we have to act like one much earlier than code review.</p>

<h2 id="review-by-exception"><strong>Review by exception</strong></h2>

<p>None of this means nobody ever reviews code. There are absolutely changes where I want another experienced human looking. An example would be a fundamental architectural change. Assuming we did a design session as a wider team, we might want to review the code as a team or agree it was implemented right, or discuss if we want to change anything. Other examples could be something crossing a sensitive security boundary, a change with a huge blast radius, an unfamiliar part of a critical system or simply something where the team says, “I’m not confident about this.”</p>

<p>Those are exactly the places where human judgment is valuable, but that’s very different from requiring a human to inspect every change because that’s the ceremony we’ve historically used to create confidence.</p>

<p>And we know now it’s not viable to continue down this path, hence why code review keeps coming up as an issue or a blocker. If an agent can produce ten times the code but every line eventually queues up waiting for a senior engineer to inspect it, we haven’t created a ten-times engineering organisation, we’ve created a big backlog and a new bottleneck.</p>

<p>And I don’t think the answer is an AI agent pretending to be the human reviewer so we can preserve exactly the same process at higher speed. That’s automating the ceremony rather than questioning why the ceremony exists.</p>

<p>There is one thing I do worry about in Brian’s argument, though. He talks about teams accumulating cognitive and intent debt: software grows while the humans responsible for it understand less and less about why it works the way it does. I think that’s a very real problem. I just don’t think mandatory pull requests are a particularly strong defence against it.</p>

<p>If agents are going to produce substantially more of the implementation, we need to be much more deliberate about maintaining human understanding through collaborative design, pairing, good boundaries, executable architecture, shared operational responsibility and probably some practices we haven’t invented yet.</p>

<p><strong>We need engineers to understand systems, not diffs.</strong></p>

<p>Perhaps that’s what AI is exposing. We’ve spent years loading an extraordinary number of responsibilities onto the humble code review: quality gate, security check, architecture review, mentoring mechanism, knowledge-sharing system, ownership model.</p>

<p>It worked, sort of, while humans could only produce code so quickly. That constraint is disappearing. So perhaps the question isn’t how we get the code reviewed faster. Perhaps it’s why we’re waiting until code review to have all the important conversations in the first place.</p>

---

### [Fragments: September  1](https://martinfowler.com/fragments/2026-09-01.html)

**来源**: Martin Fowler

**摘要**: <p>Like many readers, I’m wary of AI generated prose. Simon Wilison has written an <a href="https://tools.simonwillison.net/llm-cliche-highlighter#https://martinfowler.com/articles/making-data-ready-for-agentic-ai.html">LLM cliché highlighter</a> - paste in some text, or a URL, and it will flag various patterns common to LLMs. It references a <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Signs_of_AI_writing">wikipedia page of signs of AI writing</a>. That page points out that:</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Humans are notoriously bad at distinguishing human and LLM-generated text. While research on humans’ abilities to detect AI-generated text is still limited, a 2025 study has shown that human ability to distinguish LLM text from human is no better than random chance. Another 2025 study on German theses has shown that humans managed a “recognition rate of 57% for AI texts and 64% for human-generated texts”.[</p>
</blockquote>

<p>Not just do I find myself repelled by prose with an LLM-voice, I also wonder how accurate my reaction is. I’m old enough to see all sorts of new tic-phrases appear, and in the past would just chalk it up to youngsters or airport business books. (Not to mention Americanisms, which I’ll get used to momentarily.)</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>NVIDIA’s technical blog reports on an <a href="https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-avo-reaches-100-on-arc-agi-3-demonstrating-a-frontier-level-general-purpose-architecture-for-long-horizon-autonomous-agents/">Architecture for Long-Horizon Autonomous Agents</a>. Their research group used a combination of Claude Opus 5 and a harness called AVO, and used it first to do GPU kernel optimization and then a broader reasoning benchmark (ARC-AGI-3). Both of these were long-term tasks, for the kernel optimization the agent ran for seven days.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>AVO is designed to preserve progress beyond a single model context. Two mechanisms are particularly important: persistent memory and supervision.</p>

  <p>Persistent memory carries forward prior implementations, evaluation results, compiler and profiler outputs, and accumulated reasoning, allowing the agent to resume from the current state rather than repeatedly reconstructing the search.</p>

  <p>The supervisor monitors the broader trajectory for stagnation or repeated unproductive cycles and can redirect the main agent toward alternative strategies when needed. During the seven-day attention-kernel run, the main agent remained responsible for deciding what to inspect, change, test, and evaluate, while the supervisor helped maintain forward progress when the search plateaued.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>The team was encouraged that AVO did well at two different kinds of long-horizon tasks, indicating that it’s a general-purpose tool.</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p><a href="https://x.com/mickeynp/status/2092525399209058394">Mickey Petersen</a>:</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>MCP is SOAP for Zoomers.</p>
</blockquote>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>Paul Stack writes that <a href="https://stack72.dev/ai-broke-the-assumptions-behind-ci/">AI Broke the Assumptions Behind CI</a>. Here’s his description of CI with agents.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>An agent writes a change, opens a PR, and CI picks it up instantly. The compile fails, the agent pushes a fix, CI picks it up instantly again. A test fails, another fix, another instant run. Each iteration is fast, but the agent is still discovering that its change doesn’t work only after it crosses the PR boundary. The feedback loop is in the wrong place regardless of how fast CI runs.</p>
</blockquote>

<p>He points out that all of this breaks the pipeline, because “CI” keeps failing, and advocates doing verification before the agent pushes. This is where I get to be the grumpy old guy, and point out that was <a href="https://martinfowler.com/articles/continuousIntegration.html#BuildingAFeatureWithContinuousIntegration">always how Continuous Integration works</a>. When I’m done with a change, first I pull (to get everyone else’s change since I started), I build and test locally, and if all is well I push and let the CI server do its thing. The only reason the CI server should fail is if there’s some funky mismatch between my machine and the CI server. Tests that take a while to run aren’t part of this loop, instead they are run further down the <a href="https://martinfowler.com/bliki/DeploymentPipeline.html">deployment pipeline</a>, downstream of CI. Any failures there imply missing tests in CI.</p>

<p>(I’m being a bit unfair dumping on this article here. After all I could have filled a full working day correcting misleading descriptions of Continuous Integration for most of the last twenty years. Maybe I’m just after an excuse to point readers to the <a href="https://martinfowler.com/delivery.html">extensive range of articles</a> hosted here about what’s needed to get code from laptop to production.)</p>

<p>Stack is right that we should question how the deployment pipelines should work with agents in play. He’s also right that CI with humans relies on them being disciplined to run commit tests locally before pushing to the CI server - and that we can (and should) automate that when using agents. I also don’t know more about his setup than what he’s written in his post, so there’s likely complications he faces that I don’t understand. But when thinking about designing pipelines it’s important to understand the principles that underlie <a href="https://martinfowler.com/bliki/ContinuousDelivery.html">Continuous Delivery</a>, understand how the practices really work, and understand why they are in place. Above all, Continuous Integration is a practice, not just the CI server. Yes, CI does conflate two jobs: executing verification and coordinating merges. But that’s the point: verification is a necessary part of merging if we want to retain a healthy <a href="https://martinfowler.com/articles/branching-patterns.html#mainline">mainline</a>.</p>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>Recently Noah Smith posted an article about how he was worried about an <a href="https://www.noahpinion.blog/p/heres-how-were-all-going-to-die">AI-generated super-virus savaging humanity</a>. It’s a worry I’ve heard a few times, seen as a greater concern than AI turning us into labradors or paper-clips. Claus Wilke, who works in the field, <a href="https://blog.genesmindsmachines.com/p/im-sorry-youre-not-going-to-die-from">isn’t so concerned</a>.</p>

<blockquote>
  <p>Computational design of biological systems is unfathomably difficult. Experts who have dedicated their life to this topic routinely hit their head against the wall when nothing they try seems to work. PhD students in 2026 using state-of-the-art AI software are spending months or years trying to design simple peptide binders that inhibit some enzyme or pull down some protein, and the majority of their designs fail, or don’t express, or are toxic. But in Smith’s fictitious world a disgruntled teenager with no special training in biology can just solve a problem thousands of times more complicated than designing a peptide binder. The distance between where we are today and where we would have to be for Smith’s story to have any realism is enormous.</p>
</blockquote>

<p> ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄                ❄</p>

<p>It seems that a couple of remarkably talented  academics are experts in a staggeringly wide range of fields. <a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.02184">Or maybe they are just ghosts.</a></p>

<blockquote>
  <p>These names do not exist. Elena Vasquez and Marcus Chen have appeared as volcano experts, astronauts, thriller protagonists, podcast hosts, and academic co-authors across hundreds of independently produced AI-generated documents, never having lived. We show that large language models do not merely default to high-probability individual names when generating fictional experts: they produce correlated character ensembles: pairs and trios whose co-occurrence rates far exceed chance and are consistent across independent generations.</p>
</blockquote>

### [v0.29.0](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.29.0)

**来源**: vLLM GitHub Releases

**摘要**: <h1>v0.29.0</h1>
<h2>Highlights</h2>
<p>This release features 594 commits from 277 contributors (91 new)!</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2 is now the default for all models</strong> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53183">#53183</a>), completing the rollout that began with pooling models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48290">#48290</a>). MRV2 also gained CUDA graph memory profiling for KV cache auto-sizing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53306">#53306</a>), batch-sharded sampling that cuts per-step logits memory by 1/TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50465">#50465</a>), prompt embeds (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42963">#42963</a>), <code>extract_hidden_states</code> speculation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49811">#49811</a>), padded FULL cudagraph dispatch for uniform decode under spec decode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53407">#53407</a>), and DP-sync skipping before EAGLE/MTP draft prefill (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53694">#53694</a>). MRV1 remains in use for a few ROCm models and features MRV2 does not yet support.</li>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: Hy4-preview, Tencent's 770B/49B-active MoE with Gated DeepSeek Sparse Attention and native MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54160">#54160</a>); Qwen3.8-Flash-Next with BF16/FP8/NVFP4 and MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53896">#53896</a>); GraniteSWA and GraniteMoeSWA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52706">#52706</a>); NemotronH_Omni_Reasoning_V3 with MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52929">#52929</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53121">#53121</a>); Kimi K3 NVFP4 checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53132">#53132</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kimi-K3 and DeepSeek V4 performance</strong>: fused MXFP4 top-k finalization in the K3 latent tail (about 5% E2E latency, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53152">#53152</a>), K3 Mamba metadata preparation in one Triton launch (6.6-7.6x kernel speedup, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52388">#52388</a>), tuned Hopper low-latency GEMM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54088">#54088</a>) now also dispatched on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53534">#53534</a>) and used for <code>eh_proj</code> (12.9-25.2% kernel speedup, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53942">#53942</a>), GEMM-RS extended to GEMM-AR (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53053">#53053</a>), MLA gate merged into the QKV-A projection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54015">#54015</a>), K3 DCP with DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52188">#52188</a>) and DCP partial prefix cache hits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50493">#50493</a>); DeepSeek V4 shared experts fused into MegaMoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53040">#53040</a>), adaptive top-k width re-landed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52823">#52823</a>), a native SwiGLU clamp kernel for Humming MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53685">#53685</a>), and an opt-in FlashInfer <code>moe_ep</code> expert backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49636">#49636</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: per-request acceptance stats in OpenAI API responses via <code>--per-request-spec-decode-metrics</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48915">#48915</a>), adaptive verification extended to logprobs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52242">#52242</a>), SM100 sparse MLA for GLM-5.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52783">#52783</a>) and DeepSeek V4 on SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52795">#52795</a>), Qwen3-Omni DSpark drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52560">#52560</a>), PLaMo3 EAGLE-3/DFlash (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54239">#54239</a>), and DFlash2 loading from speculators format (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53797">#53797</a>).</li>
<li><strong>RL weight sync</strong>: a new <code>sharded_rdt</code> P2P backend where each worker pulls only its TP/EP slice over NIXL or Ray Direct Transport (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43375">#43375</a>), rank-local IPC weight updates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52497">#52497</a>), sparse checkpoint-coordinate updates through native weight loaders (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50723">#50723</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53751">#53751</a>), and routed expert loading for gpt-oss (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52209">#52209</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Mamba prefix caching</strong>: internal prefill checkpoints deliver a 9%-25% TTFT improvement (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52789">#52789</a>); <code>prefix_cache_retention_interval</code> is now a CLI argument defaulting to 0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52216">#52216</a>), with dense retention automatically restored for hybrid models using EAGLE/MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55760">#55760</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55861">#55861</a>).</li>
<li><strong>New defaults</strong>: FlashInfer all-reduce enabled by default for TP CUDA groups, opt out with <code>VLLM_ALLREDUCE_USE_FLASHINFER=0</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52998">#52998</a>); prefix-cache <code>NONE_HASH</code> is deterministic by default so distributed KV cache users no longer need to pin <code>PYTHONHASHSEED</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51875">#51875</a>); new <code>--max-num-queued-reqs</code> / <code>--max-num-queued-tokens</code> admission-control flags (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49445">#49445</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Breaking changes</strong>: ten deprecated model architectures removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53608">#53608</a>); FlexOlmo, Olmo3 and Hunyuan V1/VL migrated to the Transformers modeling backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53615">#53615</a>); PyAV video decoder backend removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54231">#54231</a>); <code>python -m vllm.entrypoints.openai.api_server</code> deprecated in favor of <code>vllm serve</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52131">#52131</a>); <code>VLLM_TEST_FORCE_FP8_MARLIN</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52182">#52182</a>) and <code>VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FP4_ASM_GEMM</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53141">#53141</a>) removed.</li>
</ul>
<h2>⚠️ Model Runner V1 Deprecation</h2>
<p>Now that Model Runner V2 is used by default, we are considering Model Runner V1 deprecated and are targeting v0.32 for its removal. We do not intend to accept any more MRV1-specific improvements or optimizations.</p>
<p>Some <a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/47172">features</a> are not yet supported in MRV2 but we are planning for these gaps to be closed within the next 2-3 weeks. These include sequence parallelism, dual-batch overlap, elastic expert parallellism, custom logits processors and certain speculative decoding methods. For now, vLLM will still fall back to use MRV1 if any of these features are configured.</p>

<h3>Other Artifacts</h3>
<p>Pre-built release artifacts are available in the <strong>Assets</strong> section at the bottom of this page, including:</p>
<ul>

<h2>Model Support</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New models</strong>: Hy4-preview (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54160">#54160</a>), Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53896">#53896</a>), GraniteSWA and GraniteMoeSWA via the existing Granite implementation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52706">#52706</a>), NemotronH_Omni_Reasoning_V3 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52929">#52929</a>) with MTP for Nemotron VL models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53121">#53121</a>), bidirectional attention for DeepSeek-backbone embedding models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52948">#52948</a>), FP8 ModernBERT (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53101">#53101</a>), and Kimi K3 NVFP4 checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53132">#53132</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Transformers modeling backend</strong>: FlexOlmo, Olmo3 and Hunyuan V1/VL migrated off native implementations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53615">#53615</a>), multimodal path hardened (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51827">#51827</a>), and <code>RMSNormFuser.fuse</code> performance fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52766">#52766</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Weight loading</strong>: weight tying now inspects the checkpoint so a real <code>lm_head</code> is loaded even when the config claims tied embeddings (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51665">#51665</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53170">#53170</a>).</li>
<li><strong>LoRA</strong>: DeepSeek V4 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53361">#53361</a>), Qwen3-Omni multimodal LoRA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52786">#52786</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53557">#53557</a>), tower/connector LoRA for LLaVA-NeXT (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49788">#49788</a>) and LFM2-VL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51498">#51498</a>), plus fixes for Muse-Glimmer (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53513">#53513</a>), Qwen3.5 embedding modules (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48850">#48850</a>), partial LoRA on Qwen3.5/3.6 GatedDeltaNet (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47640">#47640</a>), int32 overflow in punica kernels at long context (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53034">#53034</a>), false target matches on unsupported module types (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52313">#52313</a>), and base-layer/routed-expert prefix ordering (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52552">#52552</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal performance</strong>: ViT full CUDA graph for Idefics3 and SmolVLM (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47625">#47625</a>), packed encoder attention for Pixtral (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52185">#52185</a>), fused Kimi vision Q/K RoPE kernel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50400">#50400</a>), Dots3 NOTE runtime (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53517">#53517</a>) and Omni encoder optimizations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53460">#53460</a>), MM tensors no longer broadcast to workers for prefix-cache-covered items (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52041">#52041</a>), redundant placeholder scans skipped (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52925">#52925</a>), common token sequences cached (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53560">#53560</a>), async media resolved concurrently across modalities (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54537">#54537</a>), and H2D copies pinned to avoid stream stalls (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53412">#53412</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54292">#54292</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54299">#54299</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal inputs</strong>: video embeds accepted by the Python frontend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54242">#54242</a>), modality-scoped <code>mm_processor_kwargs</code> honored (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53808">#53808</a>), Qwen3-VL profiling honors <code>cap_pixels_per_frame</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54380">#54380</a>), video frame sampling respects MP4 edit-list trims (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48608">#48608</a>), oversized items skip the MM processor cache instead of crashing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53016">#53016</a>), encoder cache entries survive until their last use (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54284">#54284</a>), and repeated multimodal requests with the SHM cache no longer terminate the engine (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54994">#54994</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Correctness</strong>: PaliGemma stale image scaling removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52692">#52692</a>), Mistral3 placeholder grid with processor size overrides (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52874">#52874</a>), MiniCPM-o on Transformers v5 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54501">#54501</a>), JinaVL cache order (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53553">#53553</a>), mixed CLIP/SigLIP pooling batches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53165">#53165</a>), XD-RoPE models on prefix-cache hits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53456">#53456</a>), Qwen3-Omni audio encoder with non-divisible TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50858">#50858</a>), GLM-5.2 no longer uses dense MHA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52512">#52512</a>), Moondream3 MoE all-reduce (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54152">#54152</a>), deepseek-vl2 config defaults (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51302">#51302</a>), HY-V3 compressed-tensors ignore matching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48682">#48682</a>), MiniMax-M3 FP8 query allocation under CUDA graph replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51203">#51203</a>), and GraniteMoeHybrid quantized expert loading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54052">#54052</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Engine Core</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Model Runner V2</strong>: default for all models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53183">#53183</a>) and pooling models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48290">#48290</a>), CUDA graph memory reservation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53306">#53306</a>), batch-sharded sampling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50465">#50465</a>), prompt embeds (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42963">#42963</a>), <code>extract_hidden_states</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49811">#49811</a>), padded FULL cudagraph dispatch (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53407">#53407</a>), DP-sync skipping for drafts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53694">#53694</a>), decoupled draft/target gumbel noise streams (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54282">#54282</a>), encoder-only path split out (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53176">#53176</a>), and memory released correctly on shutdown and sleep (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53508">#53508</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54246">#54246</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54162">#54162</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53955">#53955</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53682">#53682</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Speculative decoding</strong>: per-request acceptance stats (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48915">#48915</a>), adaptive verification with logprobs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52242">#52242</a>), on SM100 sparse MLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52783">#52783</a>) and DSv4 + SM90 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52795">#52795</a>), varlen trtllm-gen decode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52157">#52157</a>), FlashInfer MLA for DSpark drafting under DCP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54277">#54277</a>), fused GDN MTP for all Qwen head ratios (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52539">#52539</a>), widest uniform decode batch captured by default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50488">#50488</a>) with memory-safe graph sizes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54418">#54418</a>), and fixes for short_conv/LFM2 targets (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50272">#50272</a>), speculators-format config overrides (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42376">#42376</a>), DFlash draft RoPE layout (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54373">#54373</a>), draft models with a different GQA ratio (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53002">#53002</a>), draft models with a larger hidden size under TP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52809">#52809</a>), generic <code>DSparkDraftModel</code> configs for Qwen3 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52197">#52197</a>), and Gemma4 MTP under CUDA graphs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53884">#53884</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Prefix caching &amp; scheduling</strong>: Mamba internal prefill checkpoints (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52789">#52789</a>), <code>prefix_cache_retention_interval</code> argument (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52216">#52216</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55760">#55760</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55861">#55861</a>), deterministic <code>NONE_HASH</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51875">#51875</a>), queue admission control (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49445">#49445</a>), KV null block reserved when validating <code>max_model_len</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47272">#47272</a>), spec decode no longer padded up to <code>max_model_len</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53962">#53962</a>), and negative external block allocation prevented (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52707">#52707</a>).</li>
<li><strong>RL workflows</strong>: <code>sharded_rdt</code> P2P weight sync (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43375">#43375</a>), rank-local IPC updates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52497">#52497</a>), sparse checkpoint updates (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50723">#50723</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53751">#53751</a>), gpt-oss routed expert loading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52209">#52209</a>), stable DeepSeek V4 mHC broadcast buffers across weight sync (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52626">#52626</a>), and packed weight transfer stream reuse capping reserved-memory waste (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52951">#52951</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Determinism</strong>: <code>trace_decode_token_ids</code> for deterministic decode replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46701">#46701</a>), per-arch tuned batch-invariant matmul configs (about 3x decode kernels on RTX 4090D/H20, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53247">#53247</a>), Blackwell autotuning with 33.6% E2E latency reduction (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53649">#53649</a>), deterministic MoE combine under DP+EP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45683">#45683</a>), and <code>fuse_allreduce_rms</code> disabled under batch invariance (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51292">#51292</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Kernels</strong>: fused embedding kernel (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53677">#53677</a>), FA4 re-enabled for head_size=256 on Blackwell (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52980">#52980</a>), vectorized sparse MLA mask loads (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52217">#52217</a>), masked MHA prefill for GLM-5 head dimensions (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53785">#53785</a>), GLM-5.2 sparse MLA Q concatenation fused with head padding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53878">#53878</a>), fused QK-norm + partial MRoPE + gate for Qwen3.6 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52676">#52676</a>), FlashInfer CuTeDSL BF16 low-latency GEMM as opt-in <code>--linear-backend flashinfer_cutedsl</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50572">#50572</a>), replicated embedding and norm fusion for DSV3 flat models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48484">#48484</a>), standardized fused shared-expert selection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51695">#51695</a>), GPT-OSS MoE topk metadata reuse (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45457">#45457</a>), tuned cooperative topk (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53382">#53382</a>), and tuned FP8 fused_moe for Qwen3.5 on L40S (+7%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53819">#53819</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Robustness</strong>: KV cache layout standardized under a <code>KVCacheLayout</code> enum (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51718">#51718</a>), JIT warmup provider registry (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50174">#50174</a>), <code>--cpu-offload-params</code> now reaches vision/audio towers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53120">#53120</a>), attention backend probe failures no longer crash init (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51703">#51703</a>), FlashInfer XQA falls back on unsupported head_dim (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53111">#53111</a>), FlashInfer prefill LSE normalized before merging to fix prefix-cache logits divergence (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52796">#52796</a>), seed preserved when a batch mixes seeded and unseeded requests (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51866">#51866</a>), startup thread allocation accounts for local DP workers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52385">#52385</a>), int32 overflow fixes in fused SiLU block quant (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53409">#53409</a>) and LoRA kernels (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53034">#53034</a>), a shared-memory race in fused groupwise RMSNorm quantization (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54111">#54111</a>), BLHNC addressing for FlashInfer sparse MLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54465">#54465</a>), Mamba state copy race (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" <h2>Hardware &amp; Performance</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>NVIDIA</strong>: DeepSeek V3.2 / GLM-5.2 DSA routed to the optimized CUDA path on all GPUs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52861">#52861</a>), PCP for DSv3.2 sparse MLA (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52046">#52046</a>), cuBLAS out_dtype router GEMM on all archs including GB10 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54048">#54048</a>), FlashInfer all-reduce tuning on SM103 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53318">#53318</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53606">#53606</a>), FA4 hdim256 on SM100 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52980">#52980</a>), SM120 sparse MLA fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51395">#51395</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53574">#53574</a>), DeepSeek V3.2 fused kernel grids hardened for 65k+ token launches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52381">#52381</a>), FlashMLA sparse decode workspace fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53755">#53755</a>), MNNVL Lamport corruption fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53000">#53000</a>), and opt-in Rubin Docker builds for CUDA 13.4/13.5 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53443">#53443</a>).</li>

href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52737">#52737</a>), C4A top-k (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52882">#52882</a>), C4 compressor GEMMs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53838">#53838</a>) and SWA q/kv norm + FP8 quant (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53540">#53540</a>), fused shared experts for block-FP8 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53097">#53097</a>), CPU offload on ROCm 7.13+ (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43018">#43018</a>), TheRock 7.14 preview docker (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49925">#49925</a>), int4/int8 quantization fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52112">#52112</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48998">#48998</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51632">#51632</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53110">#53110</a>), CUDA graphs captured on the current stream (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53818">#53818</a>), AITER metadata preserved across graph replay (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53821">#53821</a>), and improved ROCm detection under WSL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/38434">#38434</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Intel XPU</strong>: INC int4 W4A8 linear backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50501">#50501</a>), AutoRound MXFP8 MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51248">#51248</a>), EC connector KV offloading (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49532">#49532</a>), and fixes for HunyuanOCR XD-RoPE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52174">#52174</a>), sparse-MLA metadata sync (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52066">#52066</a>), Mamba state pointer overflow (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48109">#48109</a>), oneCCL warm-up at world size 1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52389">#52389</a>), and MRoPE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53201">#53201</a>).</li>
<li><strong>CPU</strong>: AMX high-performance MLA backend for DeepSeek V2/V3/R1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52616">#52616</a>) with MLA now running end-to-end (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51471">#51471</a>), FP16/BF16 persisted GDN state on AMX (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52191">#52191</a>), Int8 MoE through zentorch on AMD Zen (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44834">#44834</a>), Voxtral support (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53921">#53921</a>), C++ causal_conv1d GDN on non-AMX AVX-512BF16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49688">#49688</a>), and assorted fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54042">#54042</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Large Scale Serving</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>Context parallelism</strong>: Kimi-K3 DCP with DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52188">#52188</a>) and partial prefix cache hits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50493">#50493</a>), FlashInfer native CP for MLA decode (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54012">#54012</a>), FlashMLA sparse DCP on Hopper with MTP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46514">#46514</a>), NIXL P/D DCP for MLA models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50611">#50611</a>), PCP for DSv3.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52046">#52046</a>) and NIXL PCP producers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52779">#52779</a>), <code>--dcp-q-replicate</code> with query replication default-on for GLM sparse attention (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50382">#50382</a>), DCP fused attention fix for DeepSeek-V3.2 / GLM-5.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50005">#50005</a>), sparse MLA metadata and kernel block sizes under DCP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52377">#52377</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51031">#51031</a>), PCP PIECEWISE cudagraph fixes (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53869">#53869</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53515">#53515</a>), and PCP compatibility checks delegated to the PCP manager so plugins can enable GQA+PCP (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53853">#53853</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Elastic EP</strong>: reduced eager-mode reconfiguration downtime (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51885">#51885</a>), AOT cache reuse preserved during scaling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53378">#53378</a>), and scale-below-minimum rejected (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52702">#52702</a>).</li>
<li><strong>MoE communication</strong>: DeepEP v2 receiver CPU overhead (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51114">#51114</a>) and MXFP8 activation scale dispatch (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51398">#51398</a>), FlashInfer one-sided All2All refinements (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51924">#51924</a>), DeepEP v2 fixes for <code>--enforce-eager</code> startup (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51824">#51824</a>) and the decode/cudagraph path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52632">#52632</a>), NCCL&gt;=2.31 heap overflow fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53008">#53008</a>), cross-node MNNVL all-reduce gated by capability (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53253">#53253</a>), and MNNVL all-reduce buffers sized for DSpark (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50932">#50932</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV connectors</strong>: Mooncake Store decode KV saving via <code>save_decode_cache</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52466">#52466</a>) and hybrid DCP prefix caching (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53324">#53324</a>), externally transferable KV cache group identification (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53779">#53779</a>), async KV loads deferred past forward launch (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53333">#53333</a>), <code>kv_transfer_params</code> for <code>/inference/v1/generate</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/42644">#42644</a>), MoRIIO shared KV region registration after the layout refactor (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53698">#53698</a>), and Mamba fixes for Mooncake (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51362">#51362</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51358">#51358</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53663">#53663</a>) and NIXL (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53523">#53523</a>).</li>
<li><strong>KV offloading</strong>: EC offloading connector driven by CUDA events (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49994">#49994</a>), ownership in KV cache events (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52067">#52067</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52068">#52068</a>), P2P tier request-level offload (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52912">#52912</a>) and abort handling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52571">#52571</a>), <code>/dev/shm</code> leak on crash fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52596">#52596</a>), CPU-&gt;GPU loads ordered against compute stream (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50696">#50696</a>), <code>store_threshold</code> counting fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52227">#52227</a>), in-flight primary keys cascaded (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53329">#53329</a>), and padded GPU cache storage handled (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54021">#54021</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Data/pipeline parallel</strong>: PP silent corruption fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54962">#54962</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49274">#49274</a>), DP coordinator wake handling (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51481">#51481</a>), device sync on pause (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52914">#52914</a>), TCPStore port fixes for Ray (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53666">#53666</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50969">#50969</a>), DP supervisor inheriting the uvicorn config (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52473">#52473</a>), EPD encoder round-robin fix (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52491">#52491</a>), and producer-only EC config normalization (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53656">#53656</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Quantization</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New backends</strong>: FlashInfer TRT-LLM MXFP8 linear (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52204">#52204</a>), b12x FP4 MoE for SM120/SM121 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52018">#52018</a>), AutoRound block-wise FP8 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47434">#47434</a>), Humming MoE with MXFP4 weights + block-FP8 activations (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51332">#51332</a>), and Humming for compressed-tensors WNA16 MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48918">#48918</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Fixes</strong>: weight-only NVFP4 checkpoints routed through W4A16 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54427">#54427</a>), compressed-tensors block FP8 with Marlin (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52966">#52966</a>) and int8 grouped WNA16 MoE (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52002">#52002</a>), OCP MX mxfp6 activation emulation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52704">#52704</a>), MXFP8 FlashInfer path guarded on availability (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52648">#52648</a>), and Humming activation aliasing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54056">#54056</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>API &amp; Frontend</h2>
<ul>
<li><strong>New endpoints &amp; options</strong>: <code>/v1/messages/render</code> for the Anthropic Messages API (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45803">#45803</a>), <code>/cohere/v2/chat/render</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53219">#53219</a>), SSE keep-alive comments for idle streams (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51034">#51034</a>), per-request spec decode metrics (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48915">#48915</a>), video embeds input (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54242">#54242</a>), <code>--max-num-queued-reqs</code> / <code>--max-num-queued-tokens</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49445">#49445</a>), and pooling requests can set padding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51157">#51157</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Anthropic &amp; Cohere</strong>: <code>vllm_xargs</code> forwarded to sampling params (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53308">#53308</a>), <code>stop_sequence</code> stop reason reported (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/45807">#45807</a>), Cohere citation/tool 500s fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52175">#52175</a>), and stop string limit applied (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53750">#53750</a>).</li>
<li><strong>OpenAI compatibility</strong>: <code>logprobs=-1</code> in Completions (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46175">#46175</a>), streamed logprob offsets with echo (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/47815">#47815</a>), batched chat echo (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52529">#52529</a>), all choices returned from <code>/inference/v1/generate</code> with n&gt;1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52399">#52399</a>), <code>cache_salt</code> forwarded for content parts (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54315">#54315</a>), <code>stop_token_ids</code> validated against vocab (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54196">#54196</a>), and 4xx for client errors in <code>/detokenize</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52622">#52622</a>), malformed namespace tools (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53763">#53763</a>), malformed base64 audio (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53744">#53744</a>), and unknown chat roles in DeepSeek encoders (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53071">#53071</a>); tool-call arguments in replayed history are parsed defensively (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48922">#48922</a>), empty <code>FlatLogprobs</code> slices are handled (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53704">#53704</a>), and empty bad-word tokenizations are rejected (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53433">#53433</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Structured output &amp; parsers</strong>: reasoning-end detection scoped to the current turn (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54089">#54089</a>), terminal grammars stop under <code>min_tokens</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54218">#54218</a>), unsupported pattern+length schemas rejected (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49996">#49996</a>), MistralCommonBackend tokenizers (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52720">#52720</a>), XGrammar termination in batches (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52805">#52805</a>), spurious FSM errors after speculative reasoning end (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53046">#53046</a>), shared parser engine adapters (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52830">#52830</a>), Gemma4 parenthesized tool calls (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53657">#53657</a>) and <code>enable_thinking</code> default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52430">#52430</a>), HY-V3 parallel calls in one delta (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53965">#53965</a>), GPT-OSS Harmony strict grammar (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52222">#52222</a>), Kimi K3 reserved markers excluded from response text (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52889">#52889</a>), and unused request-local reasoners skipped (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52573">#52573</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Rust frontend</strong>: HY3 unified parser with local XGrammar structural tags (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53054">#53054</a>), gRPC audio/video inputs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53760">#53760</a>), gRPC LoRA lifecycle control (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52840">#52840</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52031">#52031</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53756">#53756</a>), <code>--generation-config vllm</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53044">#53044</a>), <code>truncate_prompt_tokens</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/48584">#48584</a>), OpenAI edge-case alignment (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53218">#53218</a>), optimized SSE hot path (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51321">#51321</a>), pure-Rust <code>protox</code> replacing <code>protoc</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52892">#52892</a>), RL world-size reporting (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53204">#53204</a>) and routed expert prompt offsets (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52703">#52703</a>), and fixes for GLM-5.2 template parity (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51426">#51426</a>), Qwen parser auto-detection (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51169">#51169</a>), Kimi K3 <code>reasoning_effort="none"</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53043">#53043</a>), <code>n &gt; 1</code> rejection on <code>/inference/v1/generate</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52844">#52844</a>), and the <code>LogprobsTensors</code> wire schema (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53939">#53939</a>).</li>
<li><strong>Pooling</strong>: BGE-M3 throughput (+3.13%, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53464">#53464</a>) and task validation (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51823">#51823</a>), prompts truncated before padding (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54364">#54364</a>), parallel arrays truncated with the prompt (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54407">#54407</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54509">#54509</a>), and batched input throughput in <code>vllm bench serve</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53213">#53213</a>).</li>
<li><strong>CLI &amp; tooling</strong>: <code>vllm launch</code> runs the serve argument checks (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52825">#52825</a>), <code>run_batch.py</code> moved out of the openai folder (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53500">#53500</a>) with its upload retry fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/50588">#50588</a>), <code>vllm bench</code> warns on a warm prefix cache for random runs (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53920">#53920</a>) and restores multimodal datasets on the <code>vllm</code> throughput backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52168">#52168</a>), and the vLLM recipes tool gained sweep recommendations and alias parsing (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53325">#53325</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53946">#53946</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Security</h2>
<ul>
<li><code>cache_salt</code> length bounded to 1024 to prevent scheduler CPU exhaustion (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54353">#54353</a>).</li>
<li>Oversized media rejected before full download (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51896">#51896</a>); <code>VLLM_MAX_AUDIO_CLIP_FILESIZE_MB</code> enforced on all audio paths (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53561">#53561</a>).</li>
<li>Decoder prompt-length validation enforced for processors that skip the check (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/46588">#46588</a>); PyNvVideoCodec decoder slot limit bypass fixed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52126">#52126</a>).</li>
<li><code>api_key</code> and <code>hf_token</code> redacted from startup logs, compile cache factors, and the Rust frontend launch log (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52523">#52523</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53625">#53625</a>, <a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53738">#53738</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Dependencies</h2>
<ul>
<li>FlashInfer 0.6.18 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54313">#54313</a>), huggingface-hub 1.28.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52797">#52797</a>), tpu-inference v0.28.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54020">#54020</a>), NIXL 1.3.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51777">#51777</a>).</li>
<li>InstantTensor added to CUDA dependencies; the loader is not enabled by default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52801">#52801</a>). CuPy constraint relaxed to exclude only 14.1.0 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/44284">#44284</a>).</li>
<li>ROCm base image: ROCr/CLR update (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53712">#53712</a>), rocprofiler-sdk 1.3.2 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53182">#53182</a>), TheRock 7.14 preview (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/49925">#49925</a>), LMCache connector packages (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/51208">#51208</a>).</li>
<li>XPU: vllm_xpu_kernels 0.1.14.1 (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54203">#54203</a>), UCX install updated (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53817">#53817</a>).</li>
<li>Build fails closed when the selected precompiled CUDA variant is unavailable (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52545">#52545</a>).</li>
</ul>
<h2>Breaking Changes &amp; Deprecations</h2>
<ul>
<li>Ten deprecated architectures removed: Arctic, Chameleon, Cheers, Fairseq2Llama, FireRedLID, GritLM, HCXVision, MPT, the <code>RWForCausalLM</code> and <code>StableLMEpochForCausalLM</code> aliases, and <code>PrithviGeoSpatialMAE</code> (superseded by <code>Terratorch</code>) (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53608">#53608</a>).</li>
<li>FlexOlmo, Olmo3, Hunyuan V1 and Hunyuan VL are now served through the Transformers modeling backend (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53615">#53615</a>).</li>
<li>PyAV video decoder backend removed; use OpenCV or Torchcodec (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/54231">#54231</a>).</li>
<li><code>python -m vllm.entrypoints.openai.api_server</code> is deprecated; use <code>vllm serve</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52131">#52131</a>).</li>
<li>Model Runner V2 is the default runner for all models (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53183">#53183</a>).</li>
<li><code>prefix_cache_retention_interval</code> default changed from dense to 0 for SWA/SSM models; the env var is deprecated in favor of the argument (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52216">#52216</a>).</li>
<li>FlashInfer all-reduce enabled by default (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52998">#52998</a>).</li>
<li><code>VLLM_TEST_FORCE_FP8_MARLIN</code> removed in favor of <code>--linear-backend</code> / <code>--moe-backend</code> (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52182">#52182</a>); <code>VLLM_ROCM_USE_AITER_FP4_ASM_GEMM</code> removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53141">#53141</a>); dead <code>--attention-config.use_prefill_decode_attention</code> removed (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/52557">#52557</a>); other long-deprecated parameters cleaned up (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53559">#53559</a>).</li>
</ul>

### [Some more things about Django I've been enjoying](https://jvns.ca/blog/2026/07/21/more-nice-django-things/)

**来源**: Julia Evans

**摘要**: <p>Hello! I&rsquo;m on a funny journey right now where I&rsquo;m trying to learn how to make
websites in a sort of 2010 style, where I have an SQL database and render some
HTML on the backend.</p>
<p>It&rsquo;s kind of an interesting journey because it doesn&rsquo;t necessarily feel &ldquo;easy&rdquo;
to me to make websites in this way: I never learned how to do it in the 2000s or
2010s, and there&rsquo;s a lot I need to learn.</p>
<p>So here are some Django features that make building this kind of site feel
more achievable than when I was trying and failing to use Go&rsquo;s standard library
or Flask. And I&rsquo;ll talk about a couple of issues with Django I&rsquo;ve run into.</p>
<h3 id="why-learn-to-make-websites-like-it-s-2010">why learn to make websites like it&rsquo;s 2010?</h3>
<p>Previously the toolkit I felt confident with for making websites was:</p>
<ul>
<li>static site generators (like for this blog)</li>
<li>static sites that do some fun stuff with Javascript (like this <a href="https://sql-playground.wizardzines.com/">sql playground</a>)</li>
<li>simple Vue.js single page apps with either a Lambda as a backend or a Go backend (like <a href="https://messwithdns.net/">mess with dns</a>)</li>
</ul>
<p>I really liked this frontend-heavy approach for these super simple applications
but when I started thinking about making something with a lot of different pages
(instead of literally just one page), I didn&rsquo;t feel so excited about the options
I saw that involved a lot of frontend code. So I figured I&rsquo;d try the backend.</p>
<p>Writing a backend-focused site that uses as little JS as possible feels
the same to me in a way as writing a single-page JS website that does as little
on the backend as possible, even though they might seem like opposites. In both
cases I&rsquo;m just trying to keep as much of the logic as possible in one place.</p>
<p>Now for some thoughts about Django!</p>
<h3 id="i-m-enjoying-query-builders">I&rsquo;m enjoying query builders</h3>
<p>I learned that I can define a &ldquo;query set&rdquo; class in Django with a bunch of
methods with different <code>WHERE</code> statements I might want to use while constructing
a query:</p>
<p>Here&rsquo;s how I use it in my view code once I&rsquo;ve defined what all the methods mean:</p>
<pre><code>Events.objects.approved()
  .for_tab(tab)
  .with_festivals(tab_params.festival_slugs)
  .is_free(tab_params.free)
  .is_outdoors(tab_params.outdoors)
</code></pre>
<p>and here&rsquo;s how I define the methods:</p>
<pre><code>class EventQuerySet(SearchableQuerySetMixin, models.QuerySet):
    def approved(self):
        return self.filter(approved_at__isnull=False)

    def future(self):
        today = timezone.localdate()
        return self.filter(end__gt=self._midnight(today))

    def with_tags(self, tags):
        if tags:
            return self.filter(tags__name__in=tags).distinct()
        return self
</code></pre>
<p>The syntax for defining the filters isn&rsquo;t my favourite, but I spend most of my
time just using the methods, and it feels super readable and nice to use, and
it makes me want to look into other query builder libraries in the future. In
the past I thought &ldquo;I know SQL, who needs a query builder?&rdquo;, but this kind of
structure does make it really nice to read.</p>
<p>I found an example of someone who <a href="https://github.com/lemon24/reader/blob/15121f667a6f2e388f0072a3fcd715f533883899/src/reader/_sql_utils.py">wrote their own small query builder in Python</a>
that I want to read later to think about whether I would enjoy using a more
minimal version of this.</p>
<h3 id="the-template-filters-are-awesome">the template filters are awesome</h3>
<p>There are a bunch of little quality of life
